\item At event cuts, 17 nonnegative integer gaps and one integer mode coordinate define an exact partial piecewise homogeneous integer-linear map on $\NN^{18}$. Finite reachability of its designated accepting modes is universal under the explicit input recoding described below.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

Here ``universal'' imports the halting-preserving simulation theorem for Morita's source machine; the finite replay is an implementation check, not a replacement proof of universality. The source distinguishes its phase-zero halt from exhaustion of the current word. Only $(q_1,\blank)$ is accepting; $(q_2,\lab\$)$ is nonaccepting null termination. Both outcomes are observable separately in $\mathcal U_{18}$. Unspecified source instructions and collisions remain undefined.

\begin{theorem}[Binary existence bound]\label{smc:cs:thm:binary}
There exists a fixed reversible number-conserving universal signal machine with ten live signals on every effectively encoded input. It uses speeds $\{-6,-3,-2,0,2,3,6\}$ and has a nine-gap integer lift using the current positive pivot speed $c_j$ as its scale multiplier. Including label control gives a partial piecewise homogeneous integer-linear map on $\NN^{10}$ with universal finite accepting reachability. Each gap branch is a rank-eight scaled projection with at most 16 nonzero entries, all of absolute value at most 12. A uniform alternative multiplier is $D=360$.
\end{theorem}

Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:binary} is an existence-by-compiler statement using established binary reversible Turing-machine simulation~\cite{msg1989,morita2008}. This release does not print or instantiate the resulting universal binary interpreter. The fully literal result is Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:literal}. Neither bound is claimed optimal.

\begin{theorem}[Canonical quadratic finite-step certificate]\label{smc:cs:thm:quadratic-overview}
For either fixed integer map, one can effectively construct a polynomial of total degree at most two which vanishes over natural arguments precisely on legal one-step input/output pairs. Its full auxiliary witness is unique whenever it exists. For each externally fixed horizon $K$, the same construction gives a quadratic certificate for $K$ steps with size linear in $K$, the machine being fixed.
\end{theorem}

For the literal machine, the release enumerates a forward-closed family of 49,700 modes and 80,501 branches, and supplies the complete numeric mode/event data, an exact sparse coefficient exporter, and a factored polynomial circuit. The one-step packet has exactly 4,196,998 auxiliary variables. It is not expanded into a flat monomial list; that representation is much larger. The binary existence result remains specified by a compiler. The horizon $K$ determines the arity of the certificate. It is not another free variable in a single fixed-arity quadratic polynomial.

\section{Primary sources and the literal interpreter}\label{smc:cs:sec:source}
\subsection{The source machine and its terminal convention}
Morita's Theorem 3.3 and Table 5 specify a 15-state, 6-symbol reversible universal Turing machine with 62 quintuples~\cite{morita2008}. Its states are $q_0,\ldots,q_{14}$ and its tape alphabet is
\[
 S=(\blank,\lab Y,\lab N,\lab *,\lab \$,\lab 1),
\]
with $\blank$ the blank. A quintuple $(q,a,b,d,s)$ means: in state $q$ scanning $a$, write $b$, move one cell in direction $d\in\{-1,+1\}$, and enter $s$. The complete table is reproduced as mathematical data in Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:tm} and in the release JSON.

Two cells have designated terminal meaning:
\[
 (q_1,\blank)=\lab{halt},\qquad(q_2,\lab\$)=\lab{null}.
\]
The other 26 empty cells are unspecified. In particular, $q_1$ and $q_2$ are not unconditional halting states: they also have ordinary transitions. Treating the entire states as final would change the computation.

For each target state $s$, all incoming transitions have one common direction $d_s$, and their written symbols are distinct. These local conditions imply reversibility: the target state fixes the previous head location; the symbol there identifies the unique predecessor transition, prior state, and overwritten symbol. All 62 transitions were checked against these conditions.

\subsection{The finite input language}
The source interpreter uses the explicitly halting cyclic-tag convention of Morita's Definition 3.3. A program has $k\ge1$ cyclic productions
\[
 (\lab{halt},p_1,\ldots,p_{k-1}),\qquad p_i\in\{\lab Y,\lab N\}^{*}.
\]
An empty current word gives the outcome $\lab{null}$. Reading $\lab N$ deletes it and advances the phase. Reading $\lab Y$ in a nonzero phase deletes it, appends that phase's production, and advances the phase. Reading $\lab Y$ in phase zero halts. The simulation theorem applies to this convention; no unproved identification with every other cyclic-tag convention is used.

Let $\operatorname{rev}(p)$ denote reversal. At phase zero the finite program region is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:program}
 P=\operatorname{rev}(p_{k-1})\lab *\ \cdots\
       \operatorname{rev}(p_1)\lab *\ \blank.
\end{equation}
The tape is $P\lab\$w$, surrounded by infinite blank tails, and the head initially scans the first cell of $w$, or a blank if $w$ is empty, in state $q_0$. Equivalently, start with a delimiter for every production including halt, then replace the rightmost delimiter by the blank phase marker. Other initial phases replace the appropriate delimiter counted from the right. There is finite nonblank support on both sides of the head; there is no nonblank periodic background.

For $(\lab{halt},\lab{YN},\lab{YYN})$ and $w=\lab{NYY}$, the source Figure 28 starts with
\[
 \lab{NYY*NY*b\$NYY}.
\]
The printed prose following Table 5 reverses the letters of $p_1$ relative to the example and figure. We use the general reversal convention and the figure, which agree with the full 184-step table replay. The release documents this discrepancy rather than silently changing the sample.

\subsection{What is imported and what is checked}
Durand-Lose's 2012 Sections 3--4 give the stack construction and reversible-TM translation used here. The later shrinking construction in that paper is not used. The 2006 result also establishes reversible conservative universality, but omits some compiler detail and is not an enumerated fixed universal collision table~\cite{DL06}. The 2007 linear-BSS comparison already treats next-event arithmetic~\cite{DL07}.

The literal collision table is a new finite instantiation of these ingredients, not a claim to a new universal computational mechanism. Its structural checks, source-tape comparison, and integer event identities are exact. They do not constitute proof-assistant formalization or an exhaustive audit of every source reduction.

\section{Finite-support stacks with a fixed number of particles}\label{smc:cs:sec:stack}
\subsection{Exact rational encoding}
For an alphabet of $\ell\ge2$ symbols, number the digits $1,\ldots,\ell$, with digit 1 blank, and put $b=\ell+1$. Encode a finite digit word followed by infinitely many blanks by
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:stack}
 \sig(d_1\cdots d_m)
  =\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{d_i}{b^i}+\frac{1}{\ell b^m}.
\end{equation}
Let $C_\ell$ be the set of all such values. Trailing blank digits give the same eventually blank tape, as they should. Delimiters in a source input language determine any required finite-word boundary.

\begin{lemma}[Stack arithmetic]\label{smc:cs:lem:stack}
$C_\ell\subset[1/\ell,1)$ is closed under
\[
 \operatorname{push}_v(\sig)=\frac{v+\sig}{b},\qquad
 \operatorname{pop}(\sig)=(v,b\sig-v),\quad v=\lfloor b\sig\rfloor.
\]
The pop digit belongs to $\{1,\ldots,\ell\}$, the remainder belongs to $C_\ell$, and no admissible value lies on a digit boundary $v/b$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Equation~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:stack} is the base-$b$ expansion of an infinite digit stream in $\{1,\ldots,\ell\}$ eventually equal to 1. Its minimum is the all-1 stream, $1/\ell$; its value is strictly below the all-$\ell$ stream, which has value 1. Removing or prepending a digit leaves an eventually-1 stream. Thus $b\sig=v+\rho$ with $\rho\in[1/\ell,1)$, proving the floor formula and excluding boundaries.
\end{proof}

This is an invariant on the encoded domain. Arbitrary rationals in the same interval are not substitutes: for example $2/3$ is a forbidden binary boundary. The gadget has exact rational dynamics, not a rounding convention for malformed stack values.

\subsection{The complete parametric collision gadget}
There are stationary marks $m_0,\ldots,m_\ell$ at positions $0,\ldots,\ell$, one stationary memory signal at $\sig$, and one operation token arriving from the right. Choose a positive speed $c$ and put $a=\ell/(\ell+2)$. The labels and speeds are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ll}
\toprule
Labels & Speeds\\
\midrule
$m_i,\mem$ & $0$\\
$\ml,\lab{sl}_v,\lab{gl},\lab{vl}_v$ & $-c$\\
$\mr,\lab{sr}_v,\lab{ack},\lab{vr}_v$ & $c$\\
$\lab{catch},\lab{fix},\lab{gr},\lab{set}$ & $c/3,-ac,ac,-c/3$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The nontransparent rules are, for $1\le v\le\ell$,
\begin{alignat}{3}
 \set{m_0,\ml}&\longmapsto\set{m_0,\mr},&\quad
 \set{\mem,\lab{sl}_v}&\longmapsto\set{\ml,\lab{sr}_v},\label{smc:cs:eq:rules1}\\
 \set{\lab{sr}_v,m_v}&\longmapsto\set{m_v,\lab{catch}},&
 \set{\mr,\lab{catch}}&\longmapsto\set{\ml,\lab{fix}},\\
 \set{\mr,\lab{fix}}&\longmapsto\set{\mem,\lab{ack}},&
 \set{\mem,\lab{gl}}&\longmapsto\set{\ml,\lab{gr}},\\
 \set{\mr,\lab{gr}}&\longmapsto\set{\ml,\lab{set}},&
 \set{m_v,\lab{set}}&\longmapsto\set{\lab{vl}_v,m_v},\\
 \set{\mr,\lab{vl}_v}&\longmapsto\set{\mem,\lab{vr}_v}.&&\label{smc:cs:eq:rules5}
\end{alignat}
For every $1\le i\le\ell$, add identity crossings of $m_i$ with
\[
 \ml,\mr,\lab{fix},\lab{ack},\lab{gl},\lab{gr},
 \lab{sl}_v,\lab{vr}_v\quad(1\le v\le\ell),
\]
and with $\lab{sr}_v,\lab{vl}_v$ only when $i<v$. All other encounters are undefined. This last clause is essential: completing every unspecified pair by an identity rule can create an output-set collision with a nontransparent rule and destroy reversibility.

There are $5\ell+10$ labels and
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:module-count}
 5+4\ell+6\ell+2\ell^2+\ell(\ell-1)
 =3\ell^2+9\ell+5
\end{equation}
rules. Thus one binary module has 20 labels and 35 rules, while one six-symbol module has 40 labels and 167 rules. The gadget uses only $\ell+3$ live signals, including its operation token.

Every displayed rule is two-to-two, with distinct input and output speeds. Its output set determines which displayed case and, where applicable, which $v$ occurred. The transparent outputs are the corresponding untouched inputs, and the listed transparent cases exclude the nontransparent outputs. This proves local rule injectivity; the release also checks it mechanically.

\subsection{Geometric proof of push and pop}\label{smc:cs:sec:geometry}
For push, take time zero at the encounter of $\mem$ with $\lab{sl}_v$ at $x=\sig$. The memory moves left at speed $c$, reflects at zero, then moves right at speed $c$. Meanwhile $\lab{sr}_v$ goes right to mark $v$ and produces a token of speed $c/3$. The reflected memory and that token meet at
\[
 x_0=v+\sig.
\]
They produce leftward memory of speed $c$ and a fix token of speed $ac$. After the memory reflects once more, their encounter has coordinate
\[
 x_0\frac{1-a}{1+a}=\frac{v+\sig}{\ell+1}.
\]
The memory becomes stationary there and the acknowledgement leaves to the right. This is exactly push.

For pop, at the first memory/get encounter the memory moves left and reflects while the get token moves right at speed $ac$. Their meeting coordinate is
\[
 \sig\frac{1+a}{1-a}=(\ell+1)\sig=v+\rho,
 \qquad \rho\in C_\ell.
\]
The new set token moves left at speed $c/3$, so its first lower integer mark is exactly $v$. That mark turns it into the leftward value token $\lab{vl}_v$ of speed $c$. The reflected memory meets this token at $\rho$, becomes stationary, and returns $\lab{vr}_v$ to the right.

All additional marker crossings are precisely those in the transparent list. The values $\sig,\rho\in[1/\ell,1)$ keep the critical meetings away from integer marks. Each construction has a fixed finite number of moving segments and finitely many marker crossings, hence a uniform finite collision bound for fixed $\ell$. Its largest local coordinate is less than $\ell+1$. The memory and operation token remain the same two live resources throughout.

\section{Reversible control and the fixed population}\label{smc:cs:sec:compiler}
\subsection{Alternating write and move form}
Let a reversible Turing machine have disjoint write states $Q_W$ and move states $Q_M$, with partial maps
\[
 \delta_W:Q_W\times S\longrightarrow Q_M\times S,
 \qquad
 \delta_M:Q_M\longrightarrow Q_W\times\{L,R\}.
\]
Require $\delta_W$ to be injective and the projection of $\delta_M$ onto $Q_W$ to be injective. Thus each target write state has a unique preceding move and a fixed incoming direction. Require also that every move-state in the image of $\delta_W$ is in the domain of $\delta_M$, and every used noninitial write or terminal state has an incoming move. The initial read side must agree with any existing incoming direction. A designated terminal \emph{pair} $(q,a)$ has no writing transition; other undefined pairs remain forbidden. The compiler may alternatively be given states whose every symbol is terminal, but this stronger condition is not imposed on Morita's table.

For a reversible quintuple table with nonzero movements, replace each transition $(q,a,b,d_s,s)$ by the write $(q,a)\mapsto(r_s,b)$ followed by $r_s\mapsto(s,d_s)$, using one fresh move state for each target $s$. The local reversibility conditions from Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:source} give exactly the two injectivity requirements. Morita's table yields 15 move states and 62 writing transitions. Its $q_0$ has incoming direction right, so the initial read can use the right module consistently even though $q_0$ has predecessors.

\subsection{Layout and tape invariant}
Put $R=\ell+2$. The left module has marks at $-R+i$ and memory at $-R+\sig_L$; the right module is its reflected, disjointly labelled copy, with marks at $R-i$ and memory at $R-\sig_R$. Put the current controller state at zero. There is one travelling operation/value token shared by the modules. The population is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:population}
 2\bigl((\ell+1)\text{ marks}+1\text{ memory}\bigr)
   +1\text{ controller}+1\text{ token}=2\ell+6.
\end{equation}
In particular, $\ell=6$ gives 18 and $\ell=2$ gives ten. The available state labels depend on the fixed interpreter, but the number of live controller signals is always one.

At a controller read, the arriving value token holds the scanned symbol; the two stationary memories hold the cells strictly left and strictly right of the head, nearest cell first. Suppose the current write sends $(q,a)$ to $(r,b)$ and the next move is $r\mapsto(s,d)$. The controller sends a push-$b$ token to the side opposite $d$. On acknowledgement it changes to $s$ and sends a pop request to side $d$. The returned value is the new scanned symbol. This preserves the tape invariant and performs exactly one source transition.

Distinct writing rules cannot share an output unless they share both $(r,b)$, which injectivity forbids. Distinct acknowledgement rules cannot share an output unless they share the target $s$, also forbidden. The writing and acknowledgement output phases have disjoint controller-state sets. Gadget outputs contain no controller state. These observations prove injectivity of the compiled control rules and their separation from the gadget rules.

\subsection{Terminal exits and conservation}
For each designated terminal pair $(q,a)$, replace its arriving controller/value pair by a fresh stationary label $H_{q,a}$ and a fresh token $E_{q,a}$ moving right at maximum speed. Add transparent crossings of this escape label with every stationary marker and memory label. All these rows are two-to-two and their fresh labels preserve output injectivity.

At that time both memories and all marks are stationary. On the right there are $\ell+1$ marks and one memory, all at distinct positions. Hence exactly $\ell+2$ further crossings take the token to the rightmost mark; thereafter it has no future encounter. This is eight crossings for the literal machine and four for the binary construction. The larger bound of all $2\ell+4$ stationary module signals is also immediate but unnecessary.

A terminal mode is recorded by the presence of $H_{q,a}$. Only the specified accepting subset is a reachability target: in the literal instance this is $H_{q_1,\blank}$, while $H_{q_2,\lab\$}$ records nonaccepting termination. Such a cut can precede the last escape crossing. If a collision-free cut is desired, additionally require the token to be rightmost and every adjacent velocity difference nonpositive. For an accepting label the two acceptance cuts differ by a fixed finite suffix. Acceptance is never a Zeno-limit convention.

Giving every signal energy one makes every rule conserve both number and this positive energy. Local reversibility refers to the partial collision table and finite well-defined flow; no uniqueness assertion about continuation through singular accumulation points is needed.

\subsection{No accumulation on encoded runs}\label{smc:cs:sec:nozeno}
All active stack geometry remains outside $(-1,1)$, apart from the controller token travelling between the center and a gadget. A center-to-gadget-to-center exchange covers distance at least two at speed at most $c_{\max}$, so takes at least $2/c_{\max}$ time. Each exchange contains only uniformly finitely many collisions, by Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:geometry}. Thus an infinite encoded computation requires infinitely many exchanges and unbounded time. A finite number of source steps has finitely many events and finite duration. This excludes Zeno acceleration on the encoded domain without making a claim about every malformed rational configuration of the partial machine.

The outer marks remain at $-R,R$. At every event cut on an encoded run, including the final escape crossing, the span is exactly $2R$. The token later flies beyond $R$, but those later non-event observation times are not states of the event map.

\subsection{The ten-signal existence specialization}
Morita, Shirasaki and Gono give a simulation by a one-tape two-symbol reversible Turing machine~\cite{msg1989}; the blank is included in that two-symbol alphabet~\cite{morita2008}. Fix one universal ordinary interpreter and apply that conversion. If its reversible description uses separate write/move quadruples, writing becomes a zero-move quintuple and a movement becomes one identity-writing quintuple per symbol. Alphabet size does not change.

Factor transitions by their target state as above. A zero-move entry into $s$ can be replaced by a right move into a fresh write state $z_s$, an identity write into a fresh move state $t_s$, and a left move into $s$. Both new maps satisfy the same injectivity conditions. The detour restores the head and tape. Initial entry is chosen consistently with the existing incoming direction, using the two orientations of the loader below; if there is no incoming move either entry can be chosen. Halting can be designated by the finitely many undefined terminal pairs. Applying the binary gadget proves Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:binary}; this is a compiled-existence argument, not a second printed universal table.

\section{The exact input loader and its cost}\label{smc:cs:sec:loader}
\subsection{Both finite tape halves}
At the initial head position, list the cells strictly to its left in decreasing index order to form $w_L$. List the scanned cell and the cells to its right in increasing order to form $w_R$. Include every nonblank cell; omitted tails are blank. This works whether finite data lie on one side or both sides of the head, as happens in Morita's program/data layout.

Let $m=\max(|w_L|,|w_R|)$, append blank digits to the shorter word, and define
\[
 Q=\ell b^m,\qquad
 A_\epsilon=\ell\sum_{i=1}^{m}d_{\epsilon,i}b^{m-i}+1
 \quad(\epsilon\in\{L,R\}).
\]
Then $\sig_\epsilon=A_\epsilon/Q$. Place the initial pop token at $x=1$ pointing toward the right module. All initial labels and their order are fixed. Only two memory coordinates vary.

The ordered gap numerators, with a run of $r$ equal entries denoted $Q^{[r]}$, are
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:loader-general}
 h_0=\bigl(A_L,Q-A_L,Q^{[\ell-1]},2Q,Q,Q,
            Q^{[\ell-1]},Q-A_R,A_R\bigr).
\end{equation}
There are $2\ell+5$ entries, whose sum is $2(\ell+2)Q$. For the binary case this is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:loader-binary}
 h_0=(A_L,Q-A_L,Q,2Q,Q,Q,Q,Q-A_R,A_R).
\end{equation}
For the literal machine it gives 17 gaps and span $16Q$. The initial mode coordinate, introduced below, is its fixed mode code times this sum.

If the initial state instead has left incoming direction, include the scanned cell in $w_L$ and exclude it from $w_R$. Place a leftward pop token at $-1$. The binary gap vector becomes
\[
 (A_L,Q-A_L,Q,Q,Q,2Q,Q,Q-A_R,A_R).
\]
For general $\ell$, the vector is
\[
 (A_L,Q-A_L,Q^{[\ell-1]},Q,Q,2Q,Q^{[\ell-1]},Q-A_R,A_R).
\]
These are two fixed entry conventions, each with a fixed initial label order. The literal six-symbol interpreter uses right entry.

\subsection{Arithmetic and representation cost}
The rational loader uses $O(m)$ multiply-by-$b$ and add-digit operations on $O(m)$-bit integers for fixed alphabet. This is linear in the prepared tape-word length in an arithmetic-operation model; it is not a constant-cost conversion from a raw base-two integer or a claim of linear bit complexity with arbitrary multiplication primitives. Ordinary binary input integers require digit extraction, source-interpreter recoding, length/power construction, and base conversion. Those costs must be paid either in preprocessing or by a separate arithmetic representation theorem.

The source program in~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:program} has length linear in its explicit production description, and its two tape halves can be obtained effectively. The separate universal reduction into that source language is imported from the cited simulation theorem, not counted as free computation.

Keeping rational positions gives a fixed physical box. Scaling all positions and times by $Q$ instead gives integer initial positions with the same fixed speed and rule tables, at the price of input-dependent physical scale. These two conventions have the same live population and event ordering.

\section{The complete simultaneous-event normal form}\label{smc:cs:sec:event}
The next results do not depend on stacks or Turing machines. Fix any finite partial signal machine with rational speeds and fixed population $n\ge2$, whose rules preserve the cardinality of each collision block and whose input and output sets each have pairwise distinct speeds. Rescale the time unit once so all speeds are integers.

\subsection{Post-event germs and first-collision guards}
A state at an event cut is an ordered label word $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ and a gap vector $g\in\Q_{\ge0}^{n-1}$. Put $v_i=v(a_i)$ and $c_i=v_i-v_{i+1}$. We require
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:germ}
 g_i=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad v_i<v_{i+1}.
\end{equation}
Thus signals emitted at the same point are sorted by strictly increasing outgoing speed. This is a post-event \emph{germ}, not an arbitrary coincident snapshot. It prevents zero-time recollision and equal-speed overlap. Strictly ordered initial slices satisfy the condition automatically.

If every $c_i\le0$, there is no future collision. Otherwise each closing edge has $g_i>0$ by~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:germ}; its putative encounter time is $g_i/c_i$. Let $J$ be the nonempty set attaining the smallest such time and fix the canonical pivot $j=\min J$. For a fixed mode and candidate $J$, the exact branch guards are
\begin{align}
 c_j&>0,&g_j&>0,\label{smc:cs:eq:guards1}\\
 c_jg_i-c_ig_j&=0&&(i\in J),\label{smc:cs:eq:guards2}\\
 c_jg_i-c_ig_j&>0&&(i\notin J).\label{smc:cs:eq:guards3}
\end{align}
The first condition is a compile-time test on speeds. These guards imply that every $i\in J$ is closing; equality would otherwise contradict nonnegativity and the germ condition.

\begin{lemma}[Complete event selection]\label{smc:cs:lem:event}
Under the germ condition and guards~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:guards1}--\eqref{smc:cs:eq:guards3}, the next positive collision time is $\tau=g_j/c_j$. Exactly the adjacent edges in $J$ have zero gap then. The maximal connected components of $J$ are the complete collision blocks.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every gap evolves affinely as $g_i-c_it$. At $t=\tau$, multiplication by $c_j>0$ gives exactly the expressions in~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:guards2}--\eqref{smc:cs:eq:guards3}. The endpoint gaps are nonnegative, zero precisely for $i\in J$. An initially positive gap remains positive for $0<t<\tau$, as a convex combination of its positive initial value and nonnegative endpoint. An initially zero gap has $c_i<0$ and is strictly positive for every $t>0$. Hence no collision is earlier. Zero endpoint gaps join exactly the contiguous signals occupying the same endpoint position.
\end{proof}

\wtag\ Lemma~\ref{smc:cs:lem:event} is the one-batch case of the chamber theorem of Part~I, Theorem~\ref{smc:cg:thm:chamber}, with its typed endpoints (Lemma~\ref{smc:cg:lem:endpoint}); Part~II proves the same step as Lemmas~\ref{smc:sd:lem:interior} and~\ref{smc:sd:lem:boundary}. The guards \eqref{smc:cs:eq:guards2}--\eqref{smc:cs:eq:guards3} are exactly Part~I's equality and strict rows for one batch, written after multiplication by the positive pivot speed, and a batch with several components of $J$ is a batch of several sites in Part~I's terms. The one addition is the germ condition \eqref{smc:cs:eq:germ}: Parts~I and~II start from strictly ordered positions, while an event cut here may carry zero gaps between diverging outputs, and the proof's sentence on initially zero gaps disposes of them. Source~12 was written without these Parts; its proof is kept as a second route. What is new in this Part is the use made of the lemma: the mode-indexed integer map of Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:integer}, the pivot-scaled lift, and the literal universal machine to which they are applied.

For every nontrivial collision block, its exact incoming set must be a defined rule input. Apply all such rules atomically and sort each output by speed. An undefined block has no branch. Thus a triple arrival cannot be replaced by a preferred pairwise rule, and simultaneous collisions at distinct locations cannot be processed with an accidental temporal priority. The literal table has only two-input rules, so a genuine triple collision is undefined.

\subsection{Why no coordinate permutation is needed}
The endpoint gap vector before label replacement is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:rational-map}
 \bar g=A_jg,\qquad
 A_j=I-\frac{c e_j^{\mathsf T}}{c_j}.
\end{equation}
Each collision replaces $k$ signals at one position by exactly $k$ signals at that position. Therefore the ordered multiset of positions is unchanged by replacement. Reordering outgoing labels at a coincident point changes the mode, but not the gap vector. Consequently $g'=\bar g$: equation~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:rational-map} is the entire branch map even when several collision blocks occur simultaneously.

The matrix is a rank-one \emph{correction} to the identity, not a rank-one matrix. Equal-cardinality at each block is important. Merely conserving the total population across a collection of events would not by itself imply this coordinate identity.

\section{Integer lifting and internalized finite control}\label{smc:cs:sec:integer}
\subsection{The pivot-scaled integer lift}
For a branch with canonical pivot $j$, the rational projection from~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:rational-map} has only the denominator $c_j$. Multiplying by that very denominator gives
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:integer-map}
 h_{k+1}=N_jh_k,\qquad
 N_j=c_jI-c e_j^{\mathsf T}\in\Z^{(n-1)\times(n-1)}.
\end{equation}
Choose $Q$ clearing the initial gap denominators and let
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:lift}
 \lambda_0=Q,\qquad \lambda_{k+1}=c_{j_k}\lambda_k,
 \qquad h_k=\lambda_k g_k.
\end{equation}
Here $c_{j_k}$ is the positive closing speed of the uniquely selected branch at step $k$. All guards are homogeneous, so they apply unchanged to $h_k$. The multiplier is determined by the branch, and no extra scale coordinate, division, gcd reduction, or denominator witness is needed in the integer map.

Conversely, a legal integer orbit determines its branch sequence, hence all $\lambda_k$. Set $g_k=h_k/\lambda_k$ and reconstruct the event delay as
\[
 \tau_k=\frac{h_{k,j_k}}{\lambda_k c_{j_k}}.
\]
Lemma~\ref{smc:cs:lem:event} and induction reconstruct exactly the rational geometry, up to global translation. This proves finite-orbit equivalence in both directions, including simultaneous events.

\begin{proposition}[Sparse scaled projections]\label{smc:cs:prop:projection}
For every branch with $c_j>0$,
\[
 A_j^2=A_j,\qquad N_j^2=c_jN_j,\qquad
 \operatorname{rank}N_j=n-2.
\]
Row $j$ vanishes. Every other row has a diagonal entry $c_j$ and possibly one further entry in column $j$. There are at most $2(n-2)$ nonzero entries. If all speed differences have absolute value at most $V$, every matrix entry has absolute value at most $V$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Since $e_j^{\mathsf T}c=c_j$, multiplication expands to $A_j^2=A_j$. Its kernel is exactly the span of the nonzero vector $c$: $A_jc=0$, while $A_jx=0$ implies $x=c x_j/c_j$. Its rank is $(n-1)-1$. Since $N_j=c_jA_j$, its square and rank follow. For $i\ne j$ the only possible nonzero row entries are $c_j$ in column $i$ and $-c_i$ in column $j$, proving the sparse and coefficient bounds.
\end{proof}

For the literal machine, the 17-dimensional gap matrices have rank 16, at most 32 nonzero entries, and entries of absolute value at most 24. The binary existence construction has nine-dimensional gap matrices of rank eight, at most 16 nonzero entries, and entries of absolute value at most 12. These bounds do not depend on the source interpreter's number of states.

\subsection{A uniform alternative for comparison}
It is also valid to use one fixed multiplier
\[
 D=\operatorname{lcm}\{|v-v'|:v\ne v'\text{ are available integer speeds}\},
\]
with $D=1$ if the set is empty. This gives $\widetilde h_k=QD^k g_k$ and
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:global-lift}
 \widetilde h_{k+1}=M_j\widetilde h_k,\qquad
 M_j=DI-\frac{D}{c_j}c e_j^{\mathsf T},\qquad M_j^2=DM_j.
\end{equation}
The literal machine has $D=65520$, with coefficient bound $8D=524160$ because the minimum positive speed difference is 3. The binary speeds give $D=360$ and coefficient bound 4320. This alternative is exact and useful for cross-checking the replay, but is unnecessary for a pure integer reachability frontend: the pivot-scaled matrices are much smaller. Both conventions have the same homogeneous guards and mode sequence.

\wtag\ The multiplier $D$ is the least common multiple of the nonzero speed differences used in Parts~I and~II (\eqref{smc:cg:eq:VD}; \eqref{smc:sd:eq:modulus}), and the uniform lift is their device: after $k$ events every gap is an integer once multiplied by $QD^k$, which is the event-by-event denominator bound of Theorem~\ref{smc:cg:thm:denominator} (with the event count bounding the causal depth) and the grid $B=D^K$ of Part~II on which Lemma~\ref{smc:sd:lem:denom} places the event coordinates. The pivot-scaled lift~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:lift} sharpens that device for one branch sequence: it multiplies by the actual pivot speed $c_j$ instead of $D$ and needs no extra coordinate, and on the primary example it ends with a largest gap numerator of 39,320 bits against 179,449 under the uniform $QD^k$ lift (Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:replay}).

\subsection{Control uses one coordinate}
There are finitely many ordered $n$-label modes; enumerate them injectively by positive integers $q$. This can be done lexicographically, including modes later discarded as invalid. Put
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:mode}
 S=\one^{\mathsf T}h,\qquad u=qS,\qquad X=(h,u)\in\NN^n.
\end{equation}
Require $S>0$. This mode encoding is used on the positive-span invariant domain of the compiled machines. An arbitrary signal machine can collapse its entire population to one point; at such a zero-span cut this particular coordinate does not distinguish outgoing modes and needs a separate convention. The generic gap theorem itself does not require positive span. In fact, positive span is invariant under every legal step of either compiled table, not only its encoded runs: zero output span would mean that all $n$ particles form one complete collision block, requiring an $n$-input rule, whereas these tables have only two-input rules and $n>2$. Each branch for mode $q$ includes the homogeneous equality $u-qS=0$, applies~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:integer-map}, and sets
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:control-update}
 u'=q'\one^{\mathsf T}N_jh,
\end{equation}
where $q'$ is the uniquely determined outgoing mode. These equations define a homogeneous integer-linear matrix $\widehat M_r$ on all $n$ coordinates. Its last input column is zero; the mode is read through its guard. The small coefficient bounds 24 and 12 apply to the gap block; the added mode row contains the fixed, potentially large codes $q'$.

The guard also includes the germ conditions. On natural gap variables these are particularly simple: whenever $c_i\ge0$, require $h_i>0$; when $c_i<0$, no extra constraint is needed beyond $h_i\ge0$. The remaining guards are the first-collision equalities and inequalities. They are homogeneous rational polyhedral-cone conditions, with strict positivity interpreted on natural arguments as value at least one.

For the literal release, give the 114 labels their zero-based lexicographic indices $\iota(a)$ and use the explicit code
\[
 q(a_1,\ldots,a_{18})=1+\sum_{i=1}^{18}\iota(a_i)114^{18-i}.
\]
The initial and final collision-free modes have codes
\begin{align*}
 q_{\mathrm{initial}}&=473631179350110573467332809270905830,\\
 q_{\mathrm{accept}}&=473631179350110518412162422097252939,\\
 q_{\mathrm{null}}&=473631179350110521664110943253890124.
\end{align*}
Their exact label orders and decimal codes are included in the machine JSON. Thus $u_0=16q_{\mathrm{initial}}Q$, while the final accepting target is the single equality $u=q_{\mathrm{accept}}\sum h$ on admissible post-event germs. The null code is a different nonaccepting target. The code values are serialized as strings to avoid loss of precision in JSON consumers using floating-point numbers.

Distinct mode codes cannot overlap when $S>0$. For a fixed valid mode and gap vector, the set of minimizing edges is unique and its pivot is fixed. The branch guards are consequently disjoint. Enumerating every mode and every nonempty subset of edges, discarding impossible or undefined cases, is a complete finite branch compiler. This proves the pure $n$-coordinate claims of Theorems~\ref{smc:cs:thm:literal} and~\ref{smc:cs:thm:binary}.

\subsection{Limits of the normal form}
No injectivity of the integer event map is asserted. Reversing a signal flow, quotienting global translation, forgetting elapsed time, taking an event section, and changing the common scale are different operations. The original partial collision table is reversible; that does not automatically make~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:integer-map} invertible.

For a general configuration, summing the pivot-scaled update gives
\[
 \one^{\mathsf T}N_jh=c_j\one^{\mathsf T}h-(v_1-v_n)h_j.
\]
It is not valid in general to discard the second term. On encoded runs, the extreme markers are stationary at the input of every event, including the last escape crossing. Their fixed event-cut span gives
\[
 S_k=\sum_i h_{k,i}=2(\ell+2)\lambda_k,
 \qquad S_{k+1}=c_{j_k}S_k.
\]
At the last cut the token may be the rightmost outgoing germ, but there is no subsequent event requiring stationary extrema. For all these encoded cuts the geometry and time can be decoded directly from the integer state:
\[
 g_k=\frac{2(\ell+2)}{S_k}h_k,
 \qquad \tau_k=\frac{2(\ell+2)h_{k,j_k}}{S_kc_{j_k}}.
\]
For the literal machine $S_k\le16Q\,24^k$, and for the binary specialization $S_k\le8Q\,12^k$. Every gap is bounded by $S_k$, and $u_k\le q_{\max}S_k$. Their bit length is $O(k+\log Q)$ for the fixed machine. Fixed coordinate dimension does not make integer-height growth free.

\paragraph{Exact points and accepting modes are different targets.}
In the literal mode closure of Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:closure}, every active branch has stationary extreme labels, and every pivot speed is at least 3. Thus $S'\ge3S$. Given two exact positive-span integer states $X_{\mathrm{in}},X_{\mathrm{out}}$ in this domain, point-to-point reachability is decidable by deterministic simulation for at most $\lfloor\log_3(S_{\mathrm{out}}/S_{\mathrm{in}})\rfloor$ steps when the ratio is at least one; a smaller target span rules out a positive-length run. For the fixed machine this costs polynomial time in the binary endpoint length. The universal question is reaching the fixed accepting mode or cone with unbounded output span, not reaching one prescribed integer point. No endpoint-size bound for acceptance is supplied.

\paragraph{Optional exact elapsed time.}
One additional natural coordinate suffices if the absolute collision timestamp is wanted. Starting at time zero, let $p_k=\lambda_k t_k$. The same branch updates it by
\[
 p_{k+1}=c_{j_k}p_k+h_{k,j_k},\qquad p_0=0.
\]
Indeed, multiplying $t_{k+1}=t_k+h_{k,j_k}/(\lambda_k c_{j_k})$ by $\lambda_{k+1}=c_{j_k}\lambda_k$ gives exactly this integer-linear row. On literal encoded runs the timestamp is $t_k=16p_k/S_k$. The timed frontend has 19 coordinates, or 11 in the binary specialization; the extra row still has coefficients bounded by 24, respectively 12. Its finite-horizon packet remains quadratic. This optional coordinate is separate from the 18-dimensional untimed reachability theorem.

The dimension retains every physical collision and is not an optimized arithmetic state dimension. For comparison, at completed Turing-machine reads a standard two-stack macro-description can use $(A_L,A_R,Q,u)$, with $u$ coding the finite state and scanned-symbol pair times $Q$. A right move writing $w$ and popping $v$ from the right can use
\[
 Q'=7Q,\quad A_L'=A_L+wQ,\quad A_R'=49A_R-7vQ,
\]
with digit guard $vQ<7A_R<(v+1)Q$; left moves are symmetric. This coarser quotient skips internal collisions and is distinct from the sparse projection frontend. Elementary stack arithmetic and the selector/copy technique are used as standard algebraic mechanisms, not claimed as new inventions.

\wtag\ The selector/copy construction of Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:packet}, in which one selector per branch and one copy of the input per branch represent a disjoint union of polyhedral pieces, is the disjunctive (convex-hull) formulation of a union of polyhedra due to Balas~\cite{balas1979,balas1985}, here in homogeneous form with the selectors entering the strict guards; Jeroslow and Lowe~\cite{jeroslowlowe} characterized the sets that such formulations represent with integer variables. Source~12 cites neither; the credit is added here, and source~12 claims no novelty for the technique.

\section{A direct canonical quadratic Diophantine packet}\label{smc:cs:sec:packet}
\subsection{Finite linear branch systems}
Let $F$ be a partial map on $\NN^d$ with finitely many disjoint branches $r=1,\ldots,B$. Branch $r$ has update $Y=\widehat M_r X$, where $\widehat M_r$ has integer entries, and finitely many homogeneous integer-linear guards of three kinds:
\[
 L(X)=0,\qquad L(X)>0,\qquad L(X)\ge0.
\]
The event map above has this form with $d=n$. Naturality of the input coordinates is part of the domain; its mode, germ, nonzero-span, first-collision, tie, and collision-legality conditions are included in the branch description. Collision legality is a finite compile-time filter.

For every branch introduce a selector $b_r\in\NN$, a local input copy $X_r\in\NN^d$, and one slack $s_{r,L}\in\NN$ for each strict or weak inequality. Form the following affine residuals:
\begin{align}
 &\sum_{r=1}^{B} b_r-1,\label{smc:cs:eq:sel}\\
 &X_i-\sum_r X_{r,i} &&(1\le i\le d),\label{smc:cs:eq:copy}\\
 &Y_i-\sum_r(\widehat M_r X_r)_i &&(1\le i\le d),\label{smc:cs:eq:out}\\
 &L(X_r) &&\text{for each equality guard},\label{smc:cs:eq:eqguard}\\
 &L(X_r)-b_r-s_{r,L} &&\text{for each strict guard},\label{smc:cs:eq:strictguard}\\
 &L(X_r)-s_{r,L} &&\text{for each weak guard}.\label{smc:cs:eq:weakguard}
\end{align}
Let $\mathcal R$ be this list and define the explicit polynomial
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:polynomial}
 P(X,Y;\mathbf z)
 =\sum_{R\in\mathcal R}R^2
  +\sum_{r=1}^{B}
      \left(\sum_{h\ne r}b_h\right)
      \left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}X_{r,i}\right).
\end{equation}
All sums here are finite and their coefficients are directly computable from the branch table. The second group consists of nonnegative complementarity products on natural arguments. No appeal to MRDP is involved.

\begin{theorem}[Exactness and canonical witness]\label{smc:cs:thm:packet}
For $X,Y\in\NN^d$, there exists $\mathbf z\in\NN^A$ with $P(X,Y;\mathbf z)=0$ if and only if $Y=F(X)$. Whenever such a tuple exists, it is unique. The polynomial has total degree at most two and is nonnegative on every natural assignment.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Every square and every displayed complementarity product is nonnegative on natural arguments. A zero therefore makes all terms vanish. Equation~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:sel} forces exactly one selector, say $b_{r_*}$, to be 1 and all the others to be zero. For every $r\ne r_*$, the first factor of its complementarity term is 1, so all coordinates of $X_r$ are zero. Equation~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:copy} then gives $X_{r_*}=X$.

Homogeneity makes every inactive guard form zero; equations~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:strictguard} and~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:weakguard} consequently force its slack to zero. On the active branch an equality guard vanishes, a strict guard has value $1+s_{r_*,L}\ge1$, and a weak guard has value $s_{r_*,L}\ge0$. Thus $X$ lies on that branch and~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:out} gives $Y=\widehat M_{r_*}X$.

Conversely, for a legal step set its unique branch selector to 1, set its local copy to $X$, set its strict slack to $L(X)-1$ and weak slack to $L(X)$, and set every inactive quantity to zero. These are natural values and every term vanishes. Disjoint branch guards fix the active selector, and all copies and slacks are then forced, proving uniqueness. The residuals are affine and the additional products are quadratic, proving the degree bound.
\end{proof}

The product must use $\sum_{h\ne r}b_h$, as written. Replacing it by $1-b_r$ would lose global nonnegativity on arbitrary natural assignments before the selector equation has been enforced. The polynomial is not asserted to be convex or a sum of squares over all real arguments.

\wtag\ Two remarks place Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:packet} in the collection. (i) Source~12 states nonnegativity on natural arguments only, but $P$ is nonnegative on the whole real nonnegative orthant: the residuals enter squared, and each complementarity product $\bigl(\sum_{h\ne r}b_h\bigr)\bigl(\sum_iX_{r,i}\bigr)$ is a product of two affine forms with nonnegative coefficients. So \eqref{smc:cs:eq:polynomial} has exactly the form $\sum_iA_i^2+\sum_jB_jC_j$ of the spine of \emph{quadratic-orthant-certificates} (its equation \texttt{qoc:eq:spine}), the format of that report's three Parts. This is a statement about the sign of $P$ only; no claim is made here that the nonnegative real zeros of $P$ are natural. (ii) The one-step graph $\{(X,F(X))\}$ of a homogeneous-guarded piecewise-linear map on $\NN^d$ with finitely many branches is a semilinear set, so the \emph{existence} of a single-fold representation of degree two, nonnegative on the real orthant, also follows from Theorem \texttt{cdc:of:thm:singlefoldsl} of Part~XI of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates}, via Presburger quantifier elimination. The construction here is explicit, needs no elimination, and carries the exact ledger \eqref{smc:cs:eq:variables}--\eqref{smc:cs:eq:residual-count}; that is what this Part contributes, not the existence statement. The fixed-horizon sum \eqref{smc:cs:eq:trace} is of the same ``one natural zero, fixed horizon'' kind as the trace polynomial of Theorem \texttt{pqc:pm:thm:trace} in \emph{probabilistic-quantum-and-continuous-computation}, which handles a different dynamics with a clamp gadget.

\wtag\ \textbf{Added 2 October 2026 (batch 79): the strong selector gates of \emph{quadratic-orthant-certificates}.} Part~V of that report (its source~16, batch-79 manuscript~09, written in commit \texttt{11abe5008}) uses the same selector device for the branches of literal counter programs, with one change: its products $\bigl(\sum_{k\ne r}e_{j,k}\bigr)\bigl(e_{j,r}+\sum_i x_{j,r,i}\bigr)$ (its equation \texttt{qoc:um:eq:polynomial}) add the selector itself to the right factor. Two positive selectors would then make some product positive, so the gate forces one-hot selection over the nonnegative reals, and for natural inputs the nonnegative real zero fibre of that polynomial is its natural fibre (its Theorem \texttt{qoc:um:thm:poly}); its source~17 isolates the gate as Lemma \texttt{qoc:rn:lem:gates} and uses it again for reset-net traces in its Part~VI. The products of~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:polynomial} lack that term, and source~12 claims exactness over natural witnesses only. An elementary observation, not made by either source, shows how far the present gates reach. At a nonnegative real zero of~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:polynomial}, if two selectors were positive, every first factor $\sum_{h\ne r}b_h$ would be positive, so every local copy $X_r$ would vanish and $X=0$ by~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:copy}. Hence, for natural $X\ne0$ and natural $Y$, exactly one selector equals one, every inactive copy and slack vanishes, the active slacks are the integers $L(X)-1$ and $L(X)$, and the nonnegative real fibre is the natural one; only at $X=0$ do the gates alone leave fractional selectors possible, among branches without a strict guard, since~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:strictguard} forces $b_r=0$ at $X_r=0$. Adding $b_r$ to each right factor, as in that report, would remove this exception without changing the counts \eqref{smc:cs:eq:variables}--\eqref{smc:cs:eq:residual-count}. Neither report re-proves a theorem of the other.

\subsection{The exact witness and residual ledger}
Let $E,T,W$ be, respectively, the total counts of equality, strict, and weak guards across all $B$ branches. There are
\begin{align}
 A&=B(d+1)+T+W &&\text{auxiliary natural variables},\label{smc:cs:eq:variables}\\
 R&=1+2d+E+T+W &&\text{squared affine residuals},\label{smc:cs:eq:residual-count}\\
 B&&&\text{unsquared nonnegative quadratic products}.\nonumber
\end{align}
The variables are exactly $B$ selectors, $Bd$ local coordinates, and $T+W$ slacks. For the literal frontend, replace $d$ by 18, so $A=19B+T+W$ and $R=37+E+T+W$. For the binary existence frontend, $A=11B+T+W$ and $R=21+E+T+W$.

This is a variable-and-residual ledger, not a minimal arithmetic-gate count. Expanding a squared linear form may create many monomials. The release gives the literal machine's numeric ledger and a complete factored coefficient description in Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:closure}. No minimization or flat monomial expansion is claimed.

\subsection{Fixed horizons and terminal reachability}
For an externally fixed $K\ge1$, introduce intermediate states $X_1,\ldots,X_{K-1}$ and a separate packet witness $\mathbf z_k$ for each step. Define
\begin{equation}\label{smc:cs:eq:trace}
 P_K(X_0,X_K;\mathbf Z)
   =\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}P(X_k,X_{k+1};\mathbf z_k).
\end{equation}
There are exactly $KA+(K-1)d$ witness variables with the two endpoints supplied as parameters, $KR$ squared affine residuals, and $KB$ complementarity products. The polynomial is quadratic and nonnegative on natural arguments. By Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:packet}, its natural fiber is empty or a singleton: determinism fixes every intermediate state, and the step witnesses are canonical.

A designated accepting mode is a finite disjunction of homogeneous linear mode equalities, optionally with the linear collision-free condition. Its finite disjunction can be handled by the same selector/copy construction. If the intended question is \emph{first} acceptance at step $K$, disallow accepting input modes in the earlier branch packets. Nonaccepting null modes are never promoted to accepting ones. Reaching $H$ and finishing the escape suffix are separate predicates, as discussed above.

The statement ``there exists some finite $K$'' is an unbounded union of these fixed-horizon relations. A uniform fixed-arity Diophantine representation of that union would need an additional sequence or iteration compression theorem. None is supplied here. Universality of the dynamics and existence of a direct quadratic \emph{step} polynomial do not remove that requirement.

\section{Exact replay and the release contract}\label{smc:cs:sec:replay}
\subsection{Literal machine counts}
For $\ell=6$, the two stack copies contribute 80 meta-signals and 334 rules. The controller has 15 write-state labels and 15 move-state labels. The 62 source transitions give 62 writing rules and the 15 target states give 15 acknowledgement rules. Two designated terminal pairs contribute four fresh labels, two terminal rules, and 32 transparent escape rules, one per terminal pair and each of the 16 stationary module labels. Hence
\[
 80+30+4=114,\qquad334+62+15+2+32=445.
\]
The exported table lists every label with its integer speed and every input/output pair. Undefined encounters are explicitly forbidden; there is no implicit identity completion.

\subsection{Replays and their meaning}
The Python replay uses standard-library integer arithmetic and exact fractions. The core stack suite checks 1,120 operations over alphabets 2, 3 and 4, totaling 24,028 collisions and 34 simultaneous batches. A small compiled binary source machine is tested on all 63 binary words of length at most five, totaling 5,045 batches. These tests cover gadget behavior, conservation, local injectivity, complete earliest-event guards, simultaneous processing, and the rational/integer branch identity.

The source-machine audit checks all 184 transitions of Morita's published example in both directions. The Figure 28 snapshots at times 0, 6, 59, 178 and 184 match state, head, and full displayed tape window. A separate bounded differential suite covers 2,430 source cyclic-tag cases, 7,687 macro checks and 169,019 Turing microsteps; 40 cases remain active at its 12-macrostep bound. A $q_0$ state alone is not a reliable macro boundary, so that test also compares the restored program, phase marker, and active word.

For the literal signal machine, each controller read is checked against an independent ordinary tape: controller state, returned symbol, and both complete rational half-stack values must all agree. The tested cases are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lrrl}
\toprule
Program and word & TM steps & Signal batches & Outcome\\
\midrule
$(\lab{halt},\lab{YN},\lab{YYN}),\ \lab{NYY}$ &184&11214&halt\\
Same program, $\lab Y$ &2&154&halt\\
Same program, empty word &1&106&null\\
$(\lab{halt}),\ \lab N$ &11&722&null\\
$(\lab{halt},\varepsilon),\ \lab{NY}$ &25&1602&null\\
\midrule
Total &223&13798&\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
All these signal batches are single-collision batches, and each has an eight-crossing escape suffix. The primary example's final exact clock is
\[
 \frac{142256492930823919}{199397583417606}.
\]
An additional event-timing implementation computes all pairwise intersections and groups coincident endpoints independently of the adjacent-gap selector. It matches all 11,214 primary-example batches and verifies the cumulative $QD^k$ integer lift at every batch; the final largest numerator has 179,449 bits. Its generic suite checks 8,092 valid germs, including 1,184 simultaneous-event batches, 352 triple-or-larger events, and 3,430 initially zero-gap germs. A missing triple rule is explicitly rejected. There are also 1,296 two-half loader cases and 32 two-entry-side compiled runs totaling 2,574 batches. The pivot-scaled suite independently checks all 13,798 literal batches and 7,282 generic events, including 4,708 events whose total span changes. The primary example's final largest gap numerator has 39,320 bits under pivot scaling, compared with 179,449 under uniform scaling. It also checks exact fixed-span geometry and delay decoding, the production adaptive-lift helper, and the optional timestamp numerator at every event; the primary example's final time numerator has 39,329 bits. These are checks of the same rule data, not an independent proof of universality.

These finite comparisons substantiate the literal implementation. The proof for all inputs is the stack geometry, control invariant, and imported source universality theorem, not extrapolation from the tested cases.

\subsection{Reproduction and boundaries}
The release has four parts: the article source and PDF; original exact replay code; complete mathematical machine data; and public-safe replay receipts with provenance links. The run script recreates the source table and its checks, the generic stack checks, and the literal signal table and replays. A separate generic event-branch compiler and arithmetic packet demonstration exercise exact canonical witnesses and residual counts on a fully enumerated finite branch system. A separate closure compiler enumerates a forward-closed mode family for the literal initial mode and records all 80,501 numeric event branches, as described in Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:closure}. This avoids enumeration of all $114^{18}$ possible label words.

No third-party source PDFs, source-page images, private working notes, or third-party software are bundled. Source PDFs can be obtained from the linked primary locations; their SHA-256 digests are recorded for comparison. The Python programs use only the standard library. Rebuilding the article additionally requires a standard LaTeX installation.

The strongest literal deliverable is the 18-live machine. The smaller ten-live result is derived. The general event theorem includes exact ties. The direct Diophantine result concerns a step and every fixed finite horizon. No fixed-arity polynomial for arbitrary halting histories, no fastest loader, and no optimal signal-count claim is made.

\wtag\ \textbf{The release in this report.} ``The release'' is the delivered archive. Its programs are shipped as \path{code/12-conservative-signal-*} and its records as \path{data/12-conservative-signal-*}, with the delivered sub-directories \path{code/}, \path{numeric/}, \path{correction/}, \path{receipts/} and \path{data/} flattened into these two directories, and its \path{CORRECTION.md}, \path{SOURCE-PROVENANCE.md} and \path{numeric/PROOF_AND_LEDGER.md} at the report root under the same prefix; the README of this report maps every name. The delivered article source and PDF and the delivered README are not shipped: this Part prints the article, Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:tm} prints its Morita table, and this report's README replaces the delivered one. The two compact closure records \path{numeric/MODES.jsonl.gz} (1,054,196 bytes) and \path{numeric/EVENTS.jsonl.gz} (580,458 bytes) are \emph{not} shipped either: they are regenerated by the shipped compiler in about a minute, with identical decompressed content, and the README gives the command (``Reconstructing the excluded data''); the machine file, the ledger and the circuit file named in Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:closure} are shipped, and the verbose branch and residual-row files were already omitted from the delivered archive, as that appendix says. The programs need the delivered layout and must be run on a copy (README).

\wtag\ \textbf{The corrected archive and the repository's review.} This Part prints the corrected edition of the archive (batch~79 manuscript~16). An earlier edition (manuscript~07 of the same batch) has the same article, data and receipts, and differs only in the generic packet evaluator \path{code/quadratic_packet.py}. The repository's Hilbert-tenth-problem programme reviewed that earlier edition~\cite{reviewcs}: it passed the stated construction, independently enumerated the complete outgoing successor set of all 49,700 modes and all 80,501 branches (the least mode-only closure), checked that the trillion-term monomial count of Appendix~\ref{smc:cs:app:closure} cannot be reduced by cancellation, and found genuine input-validation defects in the generic evaluator (a padded output $(1,999)$, a floating endpoint and a surplus slack were accepted). It wrote the repair \path{conservative_signal_packet_domains.patch}~\cite{reviewcs}. The corrected edition applies exactly that patch and says so in its \path{CORRECTION.md} (shipped as \path{12-conservative-signal-CORRECTION.md}); the shipped evaluator \path{code/12-conservative-signal-quadratic_packet.py} is the patched one, and the correction receipts are shipped. The repair does not change Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:packet}, the literal construction or any ledger, and it does not make arbitrary caller-supplied overlapping branch families disjoint. The programme then reviewed the corrected edition itself~\cite{reviewcsc}: it reread the article and the proof notes, compared the two archives member by member, passed all ten original author entrypoints and the three correction runs, rechecked one explicit integral event for every branch, and found no new defect on the valid domain. That review also proves a reduction that source~12 does not claim, a natural-domain guard projection: deleting the germ rows, the span row and the first-event rows of unselected edges with negative closing speed (18 rows and slacks per branch) is a bijection of complete \emph{natural} zero sets, though not of nonnegative real ones, and reduces the literal one-step packet from 4,196,998 to 2,747,980 witnesses and from 2,762,961 to 1,313,943 squared residuals, with an explicit evaluation schedule 7,489,424 operations shorter. The projection is the review's, is not re-proved here, and changes no theorem of this Part. Neither review is a proof-assistant verification, and neither finds a new universal operation bound.

\part{Sparse Diophantine certificates for finite mass lattice dynamics (source 13)}\label{smc:sl:part}

\noindent\wtag\ Part~IV prints batch-79 manuscript~19, \emph{Sparse Diophantine certificates for finite mass lattice dynamics}, ``Research report prepared with AI assistance for Vladimir'', dated 2 October 2026, from the archive \path{Sparse_Lattice_Diophantine_Certificates.zip}. It is called source~13 after the prefix of its shipped files. The delivered article is a main file that reads five further files; they are printed here in the delivered order, without its separate table of contents. Its labels carry the prefix \texttt{smc:sl:}; its sections are Sections~\ref{smc:sl:sec:model}--\ref{smc:sl:sec:questions} here, and it has no appendices. Its statements keep their numbering by section; equations are numbered by section (the source numbered them consecutively). Source~13 names no repository commit and cites no repository file. Its comparison and sorting gadgets and its post-elimination compiler re-derive devices of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates}; \wtag\ notes at those places say so, and Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:batch79} describes the Part. Its abstract follows.
\begin{quote}\small
Finite conserved mass bounds the number of occupied unit records without bounding the distances between them. We exploit this distinction to compile a fixed finite horizon of one-dimensional partitioned lattice dynamics into one quartic sum of squares with a unique natural witness. The construction explicitly pays for collision detection, simultaneous redistribution, sorting, finite table lookup, and empty space. We give exact witness and residual ledgers, a coefficient-level reproducible implementation, and an optional strengthening whose nonnegative-real fiber is also exact. A source-critical interface to the reversible conservative construction of Morita and Imai includes a necessary sign correction and a reachable-legality hypothesis; a fully specified doubling example is separated from the existence of a fixed safe universal source. Complementary proofs show decidability under bounded orbit diameter and, without a diameter promise, at total mass at most two. At mass at most two, a stronger effective semilinearity argument yields horizon-free canonical quartics of fixed rule-dependent arity. No fixed-arity representation of unbounded universal computation, new classical universality result, or minimum universal mass is claimed.
\end{quote}

\section{Results and model}\label{smc:sl:sec:model}
The geometric resource in this report is an unbounded integer gap, rather than an unbounded number of occupied cells. A conserved positive integer mass $M$ permits a canonical description by $M$ repeated unit records, even when the occupied positions are very far apart. The goal is to verify every lattice microstep directly on those records, including collisions and the assertion that no output has been omitted in empty space.

Three levels of claim must be kept separate. First, the main certificate theorem applies to \emph{any supplied total conservative finite table}. Second, the corrected Morita--Imai compiler provides explicit reversible examples and an interface from suitably legal two-counter computations. Third, a fixed safe universal source exists by classical simulation constructions, but the release does not print a particular universal source machine and its numerical state count. Its fully printed worked machine computes doubling and is not universal.

The sparse construction and its exact ledgers are the arithmetic contribution considered here. The circuit-to-sum-of-squares device and classical reversible computational universality are not new claims. Dense fixed-box or ring tableaux already record bounded cellular computations; a direct sparse infinite-line argument additionally has to justify grouping, ordering, and empty space. Priority of the particular constructions and the mass-two theorem below has not been established by an exhaustive literature review.

\wtag\ The dense fixed-box tableau is, for example, the finite cellular-automaton compiler of Part~VIII of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates} (its Section \texttt{cdc:mem:sec:substrates}), whose one-hot tableau occupies a fixed box; this Part is the sparse infinite-line counterpart for one-dimensional partitioned conservative dynamics. Source~13 was written without this report and without that one.

Throughout, $\NN=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$. Fix $K\ge1$, put $q=K+1$, and supply every row of a function
\[
 g:\{0,\ldots,K\}^3\longrightarrow\{0,\ldots,K\}^3,
 \qquad r+c+l=g_L(r,c,l)+g_C(r,c,l)+g_R(r,c,l).
\]
A configuration $c$ gives a triple $(L_c(x),C_c(x),R_c(x))$ at each $x\in\Z$. One update is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:physical}
 F(c)(x)=g\bigl(R_c(x-1),C_c(x),L_c(x+1)\bigr).
\end{equation}
Thus the \emph{input} order of the table is $(R,C,L)$ and its \emph{output} order is $(L,C,R)$. In physical terms the right channel streams right, the left channel streams left, and the center stays in place; $g$ then redistributes the incoming mass. The input-output order matters even when the numerical table happens to be the identity.

Conservation forces $g(0,0,0)=(0,0,0)$. Finite support stays finite, and the sum of all channel values remains $M$. If $g$ is a permutation, then $F$ is globally bijective: apply $g^{-1}$ at each site and reverse the two lane shifts. Both operations preserve the zero background and finite support. Reversibility is useful for the intended substrate, but is unnecessary for the forward certificate theorem.

\subsection{Canonical records and fixed parameters}
Let the valid initial configuration be nonempty, with minimum occupied position $o$ and diameter $D$. Replace each channel mass by that many copies of a record $(x,z)$, where $z=0,1,2$ means left, center, right. Sort lexicographically, and shift the physical position by $T-o$, where $T\ge0$ is the externally fixed horizon. Thus a record at physical site $p$ has
\[
 x=p-o+T.
\]
The initial row has exactly $M$ records. At time $t\le T$, every position lies between $T-t$ and $D+T+t$. Repeated records are allowed and have no individual labels. The sorted repeated-unit row therefore encodes the physical configuration uniquely. There is neither zero padding nor a hidden list of empty sites.

The initial data, its translation, the local table, and the horizon are compiler parameters, not quantified witnesses. Later we explicitly pay for a loader when two initial counter positions are allowed to remain free natural parameters. The vacuum $M=0$ is a separate trivial constant orbit.

\begin{theorem}[Sparse finite-horizon certificate]\label{smc:sl:thm:core}
For a supplied total conservative $g$, valid nonempty initial configuration of mass $M$, and fixed horizon $T$, there is an explicitly generated integer polynomial
\[
 P(\mathbf z)=\sum_{j=1}^{R}\rho_j(\mathbf z)^2,
 \qquad \deg\rho_j\le2,
\]
with exactly one zero in $\NN^V$. Its row decoder gives precisely $c,F(c),\ldots,F^T(c)$. The preferred full-table construction uses exactly
\begin{align}
 V&=T\bigl[M(q^3+14)+5M(M-1)\bigr],\label{smc:sl:eq:V}\\
 R&=T\bigl[17M+5M(M-1)\bigr].\label{smc:sl:eq:R}
\end{align}
Every witness in this unique solution is at most
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:H}
 H=3(D+2T)+M+3K+3.
\end{equation}
Consequently the tuple has at most $V\lceil\log_2(H+1)\rceil$ bits under fixed-width coordinate encoding. A prescribed endpoint of mass $M$ can be imposed with $2M$ additional affine residuals and no witnesses, leaving zero or one natural solution.
\end{theorem}

For $T=0$ the witness tuple is empty, and is the unique element of $\NN^0$. The empty sum is the zero constant. A mismatched-mass endpoint can instead be rejected by adding the constant residual $1$. The degree bound is at most four, not a claim that every small instance has degree exactly four. Dimension grows with $T$ and $M$; the theorem is not a fixed-arity equation with an unknown unbounded horizon.

\section{The canonical arithmetic layer}\label{smc:sl:sec:arithmetic}
\subsection{Comparison and equality without ambiguous ties}
For already established nonnegative integral expressions $x,y$, define $\CMP(x,y)$ by fresh natural witnesses $b,d$ and residuals
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:cmp}
 b(b-1),\qquad y-x-(2b-1)d-b+1.
\end{equation}
If $b=1$, the second equation says $d=y-x$, possible exactly when $y\ge x$. If $b=0$, it says $d=x-y-1$, possible exactly when $x>y$. This proves unique solvability, including ties, with
\[
 b=\ind{y\ge x},\qquad
 d=\begin{cases}y-x,&y\ge x,\\x-y-1,&x>y.\end{cases}
\]
The inputs can be affine expressions; there is no charge for an unnecessary alias wire. To test equality, use two comparisons, $\CMP(x,y)$ and $\CMP(x+1,y)$, and take the affine difference of their flags:
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:eq}
 e(x,y)=b_{\mathrm{weak}}-b_{\mathrm{strict}}=\ind{x=y}.
\end{equation}
This costs four witnesses and four residuals, with no additional equality-variable witness.

\wtag\ The comparison gadget~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:cmp}, with the same two residuals and the same case analysis, is Lemma \texttt{cdc:mem:lem:compare} of Part~V of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates}. Source~13 does not cite it; the gadget is printed here as delivered and no novelty is claimed for it.

\subsection{Streaming and all simultaneous collision groups}
Suppose the current row is $(x_i,z_i)$, $0\le i<M$, in sorted order. Each $x_i,z_i$ is affine in earlier variables. Introduce natural $L_i,C_i,R_i$ with residuals
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:channel}
 L_i+C_i+R_i-1,\qquad z_i-C_i-2R_i.
\end{equation}
For valid integral $z_i\in\{0,1,2\}$, these force the unique one-hot channel vector. A streamed position $y_i$ is forced by
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:stream}
 y_i+L_i-x_i-R_i=0.
\end{equation}
It is nonnegative at every layer $t<T$, because $x_i\ge T-t\ge1$. This is genuine line transport; there is no clipping at zero and no wraparound.

For each unordered pair $i<j$, compute $e_{ij}=\EQ(y_i,y_j)$; set $e_{ji}=e_{ij}$ and $e_{ii}=1$ as aliases and constants. The whole collision partition is determined at once. Introduce three incoming mass variables with residuals
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:incoming}
 u_i-\sum_j e_{ij}R_j,\qquad
 v_i-\sum_j e_{ij}C_j,\qquad
 w_i-\sum_j e_{ij}L_j.
\end{equation}
Their order is $(R,C,L)$, as required by~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:physical}. Equal destinations have identical incoming triples. Each component is at most $K$: a particular incoming lane is a translated copy of one old lane at one site. That statement uses validity of the previous row, not a guessed bound on the number of simultaneous records.

\subsection{A single table-row selector simplex}
Index every input table row by $\tau=(r,c,l)\in\{0,\ldots,K\}^3$. For particle $i$, introduce $q^3$ natural selectors $s_{i,\tau}$, three output masses $a_i,b_i,c_i$, and the seven affine residuals
\begin{align}
 &\sum_\tau s_{i,\tau}-1,\nonumber\\
 &u_i-\sum_\tau r\,s_{i,\tau},\qquad
 v_i-\sum_\tau c\,s_{i,\tau},\qquad
 w_i-\sum_\tau l\,s_{i,\tau},\label{smc:sl:eq:table}\\
 &a_i-\sum_\tau g_L(\tau)s_{i,\tau},\qquad
 b_i-\sum_\tau g_C(\tau)s_{i,\tau},\qquad
 c_i-\sum_\tau g_R(\tau)s_{i,\tau}.\nonumber
\end{align}
A natural vector summing to one has exactly one nonzero entry, equal to one. The three input moment equations select precisely the row $(u_i,v_i,w_i)$, because all input triples are distinct and the table is total. The output equations then force $g(u_i,v_i,w_i)$. This single simplex avoids factoring the lookup into three separate selector vectors and all their products. Its small residual count does not make the $q^3$ selectors or the long coefficient sums free.

\subsection{Rank redistribution}
Within each destination group use the old record order to define its one-based rank
\[
 r_i=\sum_{j\le i}e_{ij}.
\]
This is an affine expression, not a fresh witness. Compute two flags by comparisons
\[
 h_i=\CMP(r_i,a_i),\qquad k_i=\CMP(r_i,a_i+b_i),
\]
where the notation denotes the returned flag. The next channel is the affine expression
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:redistribute}
 z'_i=2-h_i-k_i.
\end{equation}
Ranks within a group are exactly $1,\ldots,u_i+v_i+w_i$. Conservation makes this number $a_i+b_i+c_i$. The first $a_i$ ranks have $(h_i,k_i)=(1,1)$ and channel zero; the next $b_i$ have $(0,1)$ and channel one; the last $c_i$ have $(0,0)$ and channel two. Thus the output multiset at the collision site is exact. The intermediate rank convention only chooses identical physical units, and no persistent identity survives.

\subsection{A fixed sorting network}
Sort the $M$ records $(y_i,z'_i)$ by a fixed bubble sorting network of $\binom M2$ adjacent compare-exchanges. For inputs $(x,z),(y,w)$ compare the keys $3x+z$ and $3y+w$ using~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:cmp}, giving $b,d$. Introduce four output witnesses and residuals
\begin{align}
 x_{\min}-y-b(x-y),&\qquad z_{\min}-w-b(z-w),\nonumber\\
 x_{\max}-x-y+x_{\min},&\qquad z_{\max}-z-w+z_{\min}.\label{smc:sl:eq:sort}
\end{align}
For $b=1$ the left input is the smaller or tied key; for $b=0$ the right is smaller. Ties retain the left input, but equal keys are already the same physical record. Since channels lie in $\{0,1,2\}$, key order is lexicographic order. Every comparator costs six witnesses and six residuals, including its comparison. The network outputs are the next row by alias, without copy variables. All equations in this layer are of degree at most two.

\wtag\ This compare-exchange is the two-field case of the record comparator of the same Part~V (its equations \texttt{cdc:mem:eq:record-low} and \texttt{cdc:mem:eq:record-high}, with the cost count of Proposition \texttt{cdc:mem:prop:comparator-cost}); it is printed as delivered, as a re-derivation of that device.

\section{Correctness and exact paid costs}\label{smc:sl:sec:proof}
\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{smc:sl:thm:core}]
Assume inductively that the current row is the canonical true row and every earlier auxiliary witness is uniquely fixed. The channel equations and comparison lemma uniquely fix streaming and the entire equality partition. Equations~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:incoming} fix the incoming mass. The single natural simplex forces the unique table row and its output. Conservation and rank redistribution then give exactly the outgoing multiset. The sorting network uniquely produces the canonical next row.

The empty-space assertion is indispensable. Every nonzero incoming site appears among the $y_i$, since it has at least one streamed unit. A site absent from that list has incoming triple $(0,0,0)$ and hence zero output. A listed site has positive incoming total, which conservation keeps positive. Therefore there is no hidden output outside the represented sites, no omitted occupied site, and no ghost site introduced by an unconstrained witness.

This proves soundness and uniqueness by induction through $T$ layers. Conversely, start with the actual physical history, expand each configuration into repeated unit records, and evaluate the stated one-hot vectors, comparisons, masses, ranks, and sorting gates. They provide nonnegative integral values satisfying every residual. Thus the unique natural solution exists. Counting records of channel $z$ at position $x$ and undoing the shift by $T-o$ is the decoder. The deterministic gate evaluation is its inverse on the fixed-input, fixed-horizon solution set.

If $g$ is reversible, the final physical configuration also determines all preceding physical configurations by $F^{-1}$. This does not assert reversibility of the auxiliary arithmetic circuit, nor any property of arbitrary real solutions.

For the height bound, positions stay in $[0,D+2T]$. Sorting keys are at most $3(D+2T)+2$; comparison slacks are bounded by the appropriate key difference, group rank, or local mass. Incoming and outgoing lane masses are at most $K$, ranks at most $M$, and selectors at most one. Bound~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:H} covers all these quantities with slack. Endpoint equations only select or reject the already unique row.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Scalar ledger}
The per-layer cost is as follows. The lookup row excludes the separately listed incoming masses.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}
\toprule
Block & Witnesses & Residuals\\\midrule
Channel decoding & $3M$ & $2M$\\
Streaming & $M$ & $M$\\
Pairwise destination equality & $4\binom M2$ & $4\binom M2$\\
Incoming lane masses & $3M$ & $3M$\\
Full row-selector lookup & $M(q^3+3)$ & $7M$\\
Rank comparisons & $4M$ & $4M$\\
Sorting network & $6\binom M2$ & $6\binom M2$\\\midrule
Total & $M(q^3+14)+5M(M-1)$ & $17M+5M(M-1)$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The exact counts include slacks, Boolean comparison flags, sorting outputs, and repeated simultaneous-collision work. Constants and affine aliases are not witnesses. The local table itself has $q^3$ input rows and three output entries per row; its construction, range/conservation checks, storage, and any permutation test are separate explicit costs. Expanding weighted input into $M$ unit records requires $\Omega(M)$ output work and storage. The size bounds are polynomial in the numerical mass $M$, not in its binary bit length. For a fixed source substrate, $M=m+19$ is constant while gaps and their bit lengths may grow.

Let $\mathcal L$ be the total number of nonzero monomial terms in the residual list, and $\mathcal O$ the sum of the degrees of those monomials, including repeated variable occurrences. The release declares the following literal evaluator: initialize a monomial with its signed integer coefficient, multiply by each listed variable, add its value into a residual accumulator initially zero, square each residual, and add those squares starting at zero. Under exactly this convention,
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:ops}
 \#\mathrm{multiplications}=\mathcal O+R,
 \qquad \#\mathrm{additions}=\mathcal L+R.
\end{equation}
Multiplication by a coefficient is already included by the initialization convention; negation signs reside in signed coefficients. Even a final zero residual is squared by this evaluator. The exporter supplies these exact counts and the largest residual-coefficient bit length for each instance.

There are $O(T[M^2+Mq^3])$ such arithmetic operations. This is scalar arithmetic, not bit complexity. Witness height, coefficient size, polynomial-description length, arithmetic cost, and witness dimension are different resources. The statement concerns a coefficient-explicit residual-list sum of squares. Fully expanding and collecting the quartic polynomial can create many more terms; no equality with the residual-list cost is asserted.

\subsection{Restricted row lists}
A useful optional backend replaces all $q^3$ rows in~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:table} by a supplied list of $S$ distinct allowed input rows, each conserving mass. It has
\[
 V_S=T[M(S+14)+5M(M-1)],\qquad R_S=R.
\]
The same proof certifies exactly histories whose every occupied incoming triple belongs to that list. If some required row is absent, its input moments cannot be matched by a natural one-hot selector, and the certificate is unsatisfiable. This backend does \emph{not} certify all histories of an arbitrary total extension merely because the listed rows extend to one. Coverage of the particular history or a valid-trajectory invariant is a separate proof obligation. The worked source example uses such a coverage theorem; the generic theorem always uses the supplied full table.

\subsection{The domain of uniqueness and an orthant-exact option}\label{smc:sl:sec:orthant}
The default simplex equations rely on natural integrality. They need not be unique even over the nonnegative real orthant. For a concrete full-table example let $K=2$, $M=1$, $T=1$, and $g(r,c,l)=(r,c,l)$ numerically, with the stated input/output orders. Start with one right-channel unit. Its incoming triple is $(1,0,0)$ and its output is one left unit. Replace the table selector at $(1,0,0)$ by half at $(0,0,0)$ and half at $(2,0,0)$. All three input moments and all output moments remain unchanged. Every other witness can keep its canonical value, so the original residuals still vanish, but the selector is fractional. The release checks this with exact rational arithmetic.

For any nonnegative simplex $s_1+\cdots+s_d=1$, add the one residual
\begin{equation}\label{smc:sl:eq:norm}
 \sum_{j=1}^d s_j^2-1.
\end{equation}
If it vanishes, then
\[
 0=(\sum_js_j)^2-\sum_js_j^2=2\sum_{i<j}s_is_j.
\]
Every summand is nonnegative; at most one selector is nonzero, and that selector equals one. Adding~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:norm} to each channel simplex and each table simplex therefore restores the inductive proof over $\R_{\ge0}$ for fixed natural input data. Comparisons have Boolean flags already, and their nonnegative slacks are uniquely forced once the inputs are fixed integers. All subsequent witnesses remain the canonical natural values.

\begin{corollary}[Exact nonnegative-real fiber]
The strengthened full-table or restricted-row compiler has the same witness count and exactly $2MT$ extra residuals. Its nonnegative-real solution set is the same singleton or empty set as its natural solution set. Residual degree stays at most two and polynomial degree at most four.
